docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-107-### 4. Commit and ratify a pre-measure registration
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-108-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-109-Before executing the generator under any timer or RSS observer, the independent
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-110-gate authority commits a digest-addressed registration containing all of the
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-111-following:
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-112-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-113-1. exact candidate SHA/tree and all cell-identity pins from step 2;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-114-2. **exact numeric absolute wall and peak-RSS limits**, justified solely as
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-115-   operational product/CI budgets and not as a multiple or margin over BF2;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-116-3. 30 full cold invocations, because this is a sub-second short cell and
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-117-   `[statistics].short_min_samples = 30`; the invalid session's choice of 10
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-118-   long-cell runs is not carried forward;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-119-4. median wall time, maximum peak RSS, no discretionary sample exclusion, whole-run
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md:120:   invalidation on a failed validity assertion, and whole-session invalidation when
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-121-   the predeclared machine/control checks fail;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-122-5. the exact correctness oracle, zero-network assertions, and exact-equality work
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-123-   counters derived before the performance run; and
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-124-6. the relative-baseline algorithm, fixed as
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-125-   `max(3.0000%, 2 * population coefficient of variation)`, independently for wall
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-126-   and RSS across the 30 valid calibration observations, with the result rounded
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-127-   upward—not downward—to four decimal places.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-128-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-129-The maintainer ratifies the registration digest **before the first calibration
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-130-invocation**. After that ratification, neither the reviewer nor implementer may
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-131-change a budget, formula, sample count, statistic, precision, corpus, or validity
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-132-rule in response to any observed value. This pre-ratification is the
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-133-freeze-before-measure event.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-134-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-135-The absolute budgets in item 2 require a real maintainer/reviewer product decision;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-136-the BF2 implementer must not propose or fill them. A rule based only on BF2's
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/reviews/reopen1-perf-gate-consult-codex-xhigh.md-137-observed runtime or RSS is not eligible for ratification.

docs/arch/refactor/rev11/consolidated/verter-architecture-lock-master-plan-v11.md-5762-- no-regression upper slowdown bound no greater than `max(3%, 2 × measured noise floor)` unless the locked cell is tighter;

docs/arch/refactor/rev11/consolidated/verter-architecture-lock-master-plan-v11.md-5764-- predefined outlier policy; no discretionary deletion after seeing direction;

### 3.2 `failed_respawn_retries_within_budget_and_recovers` — flaky under load

A real-tsserver test: it kills the provider, waits for respawn, and asserts a typed hover on the
recovered carrier through a retry ladder totalling ~9.75 s.

| run | machine state | result |
|---|---|---|
| in the gate | load average ≈ 34, 8 logical CPUs | FAIL — "returned no hover for recovered carrier" |
| 3-test rerun | competing with a 260 s compile-fail test | FAIL — same message |
| isolated rerun 1 | idle | **PASS** (10.4 s) |
| isolated rerun 2 | idle | **PASS** (12.0 s) |

Two passes out of two in isolation, two failures out of two under contention: the retry ladder is not
long enough to cover tsserver respawn on a saturated machine.

Not attributable to this candidate on independent grounds: the candidate changes **zero bytes** under
`crates/` or `packages/`, which is every tree-derived input this test reads
(`git diff --stat <baseline>..HEAD -- crates packages` is empty), and the tsserver binary is pinned.

### 3.3 `hot_materialize_and_script_fact_structural_rails_smoke` — TIMEOUT under load only

A trybuild compile-fail smoke test, killed at the runner's 360 s cap during the gate. Its two sibling
tests in the same file were also flagged `>120 s` in the same run.

Isolated rerun on an idle machine: **PASS at 260.0 s** — inside the cap, but with only 100 s of
headroom, which is why concurrent load pushes it over. Same non-attribution argument as §3.2.

### 3.4 What that means for this candidate

One genuine tracked-tree defect exists on the baseline and this block **reduces** it from three
instances to two; the remaining two require a ledger action outside this block's write set. The other
two failures are load-sensitivity in the runner, reproduced as passes in isolation.

Stated plainly rather than rounded off: **the canonical gate does not currently return PASS on this
tree, and it did not return PASS on the baseline either.** The lock record does not claim a green
gate. It claims what the commands actually did, which is the standard
`CLAUDE.md`'s *Verification Must Prove Execution* rule sets — exit status alone is neither a pass nor,
by itself, a failure worth acting on until each failure is attributed.

## 4. The two rows that needed a build this worktree did not have

`pnpm test` initially failed at `@verter/native`'s `pretest` → `ensure-native-loader`: a fresh
worktree has no built `.node` binding, because `pnpm install` does not produce one. That is an
environment prerequisite, not a result — the same class as the TypeScript-plugin dist the gate's own
preflight fails closed on, and precisely the kind of "green command that proved nothing" the baseline
contract warns about, inverted.

So `pnpm run build:native` was run and `pnpm test` re-run. Both outcomes are recorded in §5 with the
same honesty as §3: if the re-run does not pass, it is reported as a failure and classified, not
dropped.

## 5. Re-run outcomes after the native build

See [`command-proofs-native.md`](command-proofs-native.md).

## Exclusive-lease verification (the invalidation cause, addressed head-on)

Verified IMMEDIATELY before the session start, by direct process
inspection (`ps aux` sorted by CPU, sampled repeatedly over several
minutes), not assumption:

- Zero builds, test suites, benchmarks, or other agent/review sessions
  running. The only other Claude session on the machine was idle
  (intermittent ≤10% of one core, no spawned work).
- Spotlight (`spotlightknowledged.updater`) was initially indexing this
  session's own freshly-created files (bursts to ~50–80% of one core); the
  session was DELAYED until six consecutive 5-second samples read 0.0%.
- Residual disclosed load: `RustDesk` (a resident remote-desktop daemon,
  not stoppable from this session) idling at ~10–30% of ONE core on an
  8-core machine, plus WindowServer/Terminal UI noise. Nothing else above
  20% of a core at any sampled point.
- AC power ("AC attached"), load averages ~1.9/8 cores at session start.

Post-hoc corroboration that the window was genuinely quiet: this
session's wall-time coefficient of variation is 1.2565% — TIGHTER than
the frozen BF1 baseline session's own 2.3388% and the tightest of any
session recorded for these cells. A perturbed session shows the opposite.

#!/bin/bash
# Final-candidate measurement session for the two locked cells
# BF2_VUE_ORACLE_MANIFEST_GENERATE / BF2_SVELTE_ORACLE_MANIFEST_GENERATE.
# Runs the frozen, unmodified generate-official-case-manifests.mjs (git blob
# b61404de48e8ba86767a09414195b67a06ac56be) under the committed deny-network
# sandbox profile (git blob 5d41a32d8ba2ac7bfe905d87b406ea8f234de519), N
# times, capturing wall time (real, /usr/bin/time -l) and peak RSS per run,
# and verifying the deterministic correctness oracle (exact row counts +
# byte-identical TSVs vs the committed manifests) on every run.
set -euo pipefail

REPO=/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter-bf2-fix2
SCRATCH=/tmp/bf2-perf-pass4-final
SANDBOX_PROFILE="$SCRATCH/deny-network.sb"
VUE_SOURCE="$REPO/packages/framework-conformance-harness/.oracle-checkouts/vue"
SVELTE_SOURCE="$REPO/packages/framework-conformance-harness/.oracle-checkouts/svelte"
VUE_MODULES="$SCRATCH/vue-oracle"
SCRIPT="$REPO/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/framework-conformance/generate-official-case-manifests.mjs"
COMMITTED_VUE_TSV="$REPO/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/framework-conformance/vue-official-cases.tsv"
COMMITTED_SVELTE_TSV="$REPO/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/framework-conformance/svelte-official-cases.tsv"

N=${1:-10}
OUT_LOG="$SCRATCH/session-raw.log"
: > "$OUT_LOG"

echo "session_start_utc=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "node_version=$(node --version)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "runs_requested=$N" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "candidate_head=$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse HEAD)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "vue_source_head=$(git -C "$VUE_SOURCE" rev-parse HEAD)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "svelte_source_head=$(git -C "$SVELTE_SOURCE" rev-parse HEAD)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "script_blob=$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse HEAD:docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/framework-conformance/generate-official-case-manifests.mjs)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
echo "sandbox_profile_sha=$(shasum -a 1 "$SANDBOX_PROFILE" | awk '{print $1}')" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"

# Zero-network control: curl must FAIL under the identical profile.
set +e
sandbox-exec -f "$SANDBOX_PROFILE" curl -sS -m 3 https://example.com > /dev/null 2>&1
CURL_STATUS=$?
set -e
echo "curl_denial_control_exit=$CURL_STATUS (nonzero=denied, required)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
if [ "$CURL_STATUS" -eq 0 ]; then
  echo "SANDBOX NOT DENYING NETWORK — aborting" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
  exit 1
fi

for i in $(seq 1 "$N"); do
  RUN_OUT="$SCRATCH/run-$i"
  rm -rf "$RUN_OUT"
  mkdir -p "$RUN_OUT"
  TIME_FILE="$SCRATCH/time-$i.txt"
  STDOUT_FILE="$SCRATCH/stdout-$i.txt"

  set +e
  /usr/bin/time -l sandbox-exec -f "$SANDBOX_PROFILE" node "$SCRIPT" \
    --vue-source "$VUE_SOURCE" \
    --svelte-source "$SVELTE_SOURCE" \
    --vue-modules "$VUE_MODULES" \
    --out-dir "$RUN_OUT" \
    > "$STDOUT_FILE" 2> "$TIME_FILE"
  STATUS=$?
  set -e

  if [ "$STATUS" -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "run_$i status=FAIL exit=$STATUS" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
    cat "$TIME_FILE" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
    exit 1
  fi

  COUNTS=$(cat "$STDOUT_FILE")
  if ! diff -q "$RUN_OUT/vue-official-cases.tsv" "$COMMITTED_VUE_TSV" > /dev/null; then
    echo "run_$i status=FAIL reason=vue_tsv_mismatch" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
    exit 1
  fi
  if ! diff -q "$RUN_OUT/svelte-official-cases.tsv" "$COMMITTED_SVELTE_TSV" > /dev/null; then
    echo "run_$i status=FAIL reason=svelte_tsv_mismatch" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"
    exit 1
  fi

  REAL=$(grep "real" "$TIME_FILE" | awk '{print $1}')
  MAXRSS=$(grep "maximum resident set size" "$TIME_FILE" | awk '{print $1}')

  echo "run_$i status=OK counts=$COUNTS real_s=$REAL maxrss_bytes=$MAXRSS" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"

  rm -rf "$RUN_OUT"
done

echo "session_end_utc=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" | tee -a "$OUT_LOG"

Real execution evidence for the two locked `performance-gates.toml` cells,

directory and the final report/test-suite records — the measured tool and
all measured inputs are byte-identical at both trees). This replaces
cell-existence/schema-validity as the pass evidence: the cells were
EXECUTED and the raw session output is committed here (`session-raw.txt`;
driver `run-session.sh`, adapted from the BF1 session driver at
`../../../framework-conformance/command-proofs/bf2-oracle-manifest-generate/`
with only path updates).

The separate `BF2_OFFICIAL_COMPILER_INVOCATION_GOLDEN_GENERATE` row is NOT
covered here and remains OPEN under its existing debt disposition
(`../../debt-BF2-perf-gate-deferred.md`) — untouched by this session.

## Correctness oracle — applied on EVERY run (live, would abort the session)

All 10 runs: stdout exactly `{"vue_rows":2003,"svelte_rows":3457}` AND
byte-identical `diff` of both produced TSVs against the committed
`vue-official-cases.tsv` / `svelte-official-cases.tsv` — whose SHA-256
(`30123a6d…95bd` / `c251be5b…7a8e`) are exactly both cells'
`output_oracle` values. Byte-identity therefore also reproduces every
work counter both cells lock (Vue: 5 suites — compiler-core 570,
compiler-dom 137, compiler-sfc 509, compiler-ssr 134, compiler-vapor 653;
2003/2003 `blocked`. Svelte: 22 suites; 3313 `blocked` + 144

# Verter Revision 11 — Orchestrator Entry Point

**Use this file as the first package instruction for the orchestrator.**  
**Canonical input:** the extracted Revision 11 split package plus an actual local checkout of `pikax/verter`.  
**Initial authorization:** execute `A0` only.

# 1. Your role

You are the implementation orchestrator, not the maintainer and not an architecture authority.

Your job is to:

- validate the release package and actual checkout;
- maintain exact program, branch, stack, candidate, evidence, and review state;
- execute only the next legal bounded block;
- delegate substantial independent work through immutable context packets;
- stop and report when source or evidence contradicts the plan;
- recommend acceptance only from reproducible evidence.

You must not silently change architecture, weaken a gate, widen a charter, self-approve, or treat your own summary as independent review.

# 2. Opus adapter identity

The supplied Opus bootstrap requests fixed model ID `claude-opus-5`. Record the actual model, provider, and any fallback/substitution in `program-state.toml` before repository mutation.

If the actual runtime differs from the requested runtime, you may complete non-mutating package/repository inspection, but return `A0 BLOCKED` before representing the result as an Opus-governed Foundational outcome unless the designated maintainer records an explicit exception. Model brand does not replace role independence or evidence.

# 3. Read before acting

Read, in this order:

1. `README.md`
2. `contracts/agent-orchestration.md`
3. `contracts/stacked-prs.md`
4. `contracts/package-publication.md`
5. `contracts/architecture-falsification.md`
6. `program-dag.toml`
7. `governance.md`
8. `charters/A0.md`
9. `contracts/baseline-lock.md`
10. `contracts/current-tree-reconciliation.md`
11. the A0-relevant sections of `verification.md`

Do not load the entire package into every worker context. Give each worker only its bounded context packet and directly relevant authority/evidence.

# 4. First commands

From the extracted package:

```bash
python3 tools/validate_package.py
python3 tools/selftest_orchestration.py
claude --version
python3 tools/validate_program_state.py \
  --dag program-dag.toml \
  --state templates/program-state.template.toml \
  --mode template
python3 tools/validate_stack_window.py \
  --dag program-dag.toml \
  --stack templates/stack-window.template.toml \
  --mode template
python3 tools/validate_landing_equivalence.py \
  --proof templates/landing-equivalence.template.toml \
  --mode template
```

From the actual Verter checkout, collect without mutation:

```bash
git rev-parse --show-toplevel
git remote -v
git branch --show-current
git rev-parse HEAD
git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}
git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all
git worktree list --porcelain
git submodule status --recursive || true
git log -1 --decorate=full --stat
```

Enumerate local/remote architecture-affecting branches and open/queued pull requests using the repository's available GitHub tooling. Record unavailable permissions or APIs rather than assuming a clean state.

Initialize a repository-local ignored or external evidence directory and copy `templates/program-state.template.toml` to `program-state.toml`. Set top-level `status = "ACTIVE"`, resolve every A0-required field, then run:

```bash
python3 <REV11_PACKAGE>/tools/validate_program_state.py \
  --dag <REV11_PACKAGE>/program-dag.toml \
  --state <REPO_EVIDENCE>/program-state.toml \
  --mode live
```

# 5. Execute only A0

A0 produces:

- exact entry checkout SHA/tree and dirty/untracked/submodule/worktree state;
- remote, branch, target-lineage, and toolchain identity;
- Revision 11 package, release, and DAG digests;
- open PR/branch/queued-change disposition, including changes touching compiler, TypeInfo, flow, source ownership, CSS, framework, LSP/provider, protocol, cache, persistence, or lifetime boundaries;
- available command, CI, branch protection, merge queue, stack tooling, signing, and permission facts;
- designated maintainer and orchestrator identities;
- requested/actual model, orchestrator runtime/version, provider, and fallback state;
- initial validated program state with only legal next work exposed;
- A0 context/evidence packet and exact review state.

Do not start `A1` or any production cutover until A0 has been accepted under `governance.md`.

# 6. Delegation

Use no subagent when A0 can be completed directly with a small number of repository/tool calls.

When delegation is genuinely useful:

- default to no more than three active worker contexts;
- assign one role and one immutable context packet per worker;
- use separate writable worktrees/branches;
- do not ask workers to “review the entire architecture”;
- do not use a subagent merely to echo or generically double-check your own conclusion;
- keep conformance, architecture, and adversarial mandates distinct where required.

# 7. Stacked PR policy

Stacked PRs are allowed only under `contracts/stacked-prs.md`. Every `ACTIVE` or later stack snapshot is validated both against its pre-stack program-state basis and the current program-state ledger.

Do not create a program-wide stack. During A0, record whether GitHub native stacks, ordinary dependent PRs, merge queues, and signed rebases are available. A6 selects the operational tooling and locks a bounded stack-window policy. The default maximum is four open review layers; the permitted A6 range is two through six.

Private atomic layers, especially D1, are never independently mergeable to trunk.

# 8. Stop instead of improvising

Stop and report `BLOCKED` or `RESCOPE_REQUIRED` when:

- release checksum or package validation fails;
- the checkout is dirty or contains unowned work;
- target trunk differs from the expected line and the difference is not dispositioned;
- an open PR/branch competes for an authority;
- required repository/GitHub/CI facts cannot be obtained;
- source disproves an architectural assumption;
- A0 would require production ownership changes;
- the maintainer identity or acceptance path is absent;

- **Item 2, VDOM regressions:** directly in scope. BV0 must restore both formerly passing routes and delete the two waiver rows. The charter requires proof that unaffected routes retain their previous successful contract and forbids substituting a waiver ([BV0 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BV0.md:32), [conformance finding](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-conformance.md:195)).

- **Item 3, obsolete SSR document:** directly in scope. Delete the document and clean its references; do not rewrite it again. Its own deletion criterion was satisfied, and retaining the contradiction violates BV0’s “no Vue tracking/backlog/waiver/retraction artifact” exit ([adversarial finding](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-adversarial.md:265)).

- **Item 4, candidate-caused corpus regressions:** directly in BV0’s required closure. “Preserving every public route’s existing successful result contract” is broader than the 36 positive seed cells ([BV0 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BV0.md:8)). Fix the candidate-introduced Vapor root-cause classes across every affected corpus cell; do not patch fixture rows individually or attempt to close all pre-existing BV1 divergences. Recompute attribution after the oracle repair—the present +318/+85% figure was measured against defective goldens and is evidence of a problem, not a stable acceptance baseline ([adversarial attribution](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-adversarial.md:180)).

- **Item 5, skipped axes:** BV0 owns making its authoritative seed-matrix acceptance fail closed whenever any required link/runtime axis skips, and owns proving zero skips in CI/fresh-checkout evidence. That follows directly from its exit requiring the isolated install so link checks genuinely execute ([BV0 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BV0.md:44)). The demonstrated false-green behavior is blocking ([conformance finding](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-conformance.md:236)).  
  However, changing generic `check-candidate.mjs` skip semantics is BF2-owned. BV0 can—and must—reject the returned skip status in `official_seed_matrix.rs`. If a reusable authoritative/fail-closed harness mode is desired, include that in the authorized BF2 reopen.

## Q4 — Additional scope risks

The reopen packet should also resolve these points:

1. **Source-map acceptance was silently narrowed.** The architecture review found that candidate-vs-official mapping comparison became candidate self-consistency even though BV0 expressly owns source-map differences after harness artifacts are removed ([architecture review](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-architecture.md:133)). Default ruling: remove the harness artifact and restore the promised comparison. Any narrowing requires explicit maintainer ratification; it cannot ride silently in the relanding.

2. **Fix the full Vapor wrapper contract, including the TS branch.** Restoring `__vapor: true` for JS alone is incomplete; non-SSR TS Vapor uses `defineVaporComponent`. Invert/delete the wrong unit test and add positive and negative controls for both language branches.

3. **Strengthen behavioral discrimination.** The Vapor runtime test missed wrong sibling insertion order even though the structural comparator caught it ([adversarial review](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-adversarial.md:281)). Assert exact DOM/order, not mere containment.

4. **Clear known false rationale.** Remove the incorrect production `/* CACHED */` explanation/branch and stale SSR `_ctx` comment identified by the reviewers; do not reland code or documentation already known to contradict the oracle.

5. **Rerun affected BF2 performance and provenance gates.** Changing invocation behavior and regenerating a large golden domain invalidates the corresponding generation measurements/digests. Existing thresholds remain locked; this is rerun/re-attestation, not post-result recalibration.

So the permissible “single comprehensive cycle” is one coordinated campaign with separate authority layers:

```text
BF2 oracle repair + affected golden regeneration + fresh BF2 acceptance
    → BV0 rebase and comprehensive compiler/evidence correction
    → fresh three-mandate BV0 review and landing

Svelte migration: separate authorized block/commit
```

A single undifferentiated BV0 commit containing the harness repair, compiler fixes, regenerated expectations, and Svelte migration should not be accepted.
tokens used
181 808
The correct scope is a coordinated BF2→BV0 reopen, not one enlarged BV0 commit. The Svelte migration must remain separate.

## Q1 — Oracle invocation and accepted predecessors

**Ruling: fixing `invoke-vue-oracle.mjs` and regenerating affected official goldens is BF2-owned work. It requires a formal BF2 reopen, not unilateral BV0 scope.**

BF2 expressly owns “offline official compiler invocation and immutable golden generation,” Vue script/template assembly, runtime validation, and mutation tests ([BF2 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BF2.md:11)). BV0 is a successor that consumes that evidence and owns compiler source corrections ([BV0 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BV0.md:13)). Letting the candidate-under-test repair and regenerate its own oracle inside BV0 would blur exactly that separation.

This is also the established BF2 reopen precedent: defects in the oracle/validator must be fixed in BF2 because later blocks are judged by it; deferring the correction poisons downstream evidence ([second-reopen ruling](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/second-reopen-ruling.md:25)).

The comprehensive BF2 correction must cover the entire option-propagation class:

- Carry the complete semantic profile into every official phase, not merely add `vapor: true` at one call site.
- Pass `vapor` and `templateOptions.ssr` correctly to `compileScript`.
- Verify parse, `compileScript`, `compileTemplate`, assembly, golden provenance, and runtime all use the same requested axes.
- Regenerate every affected official golden across the corpus. Because `ssr` was also omitted from `compileScript`, scope cannot be limited to Vapor: regenerate all Vapor and SSR/non-inline script-bearing products whose official output changes.
- Add independent JS and TS controls for `__vapor`, `defineVaporComponent`, SSR behavior, VDOM negatives, and runtime interop. The adversarial report establishes that candidate and golden were wrong identically and that the existing test locks in the defect ([adversarial review](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-adversarial.md:103), [finding](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-adversarial.md:252)).

**Acceptance effects:**

- **BF2:** its historical acceptance record remains an historical fact, but its present authority and evidence are invalidated. Reopen BF2, mark its reviews/evidence invalid, repair it, and obtain fresh conformance, architecture, and adversarial PASS on one exact candidate. This is a real oracle-basis change, so use a full fresh review, not a narrow BV0 recheck.
- **BF1:** do **not** reopen automatically. BF1 locked the domain and option contract before the harness existed and expressly did not implement the harness ([BF1 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BF1.md:6)). Its inventory already says `compileScript.vapor` and `compileScript.templateOptions` are derived from the canonical request ([Vue options](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/framework-conformance/vue-options.tsv:72)). Thus BF2 violated the valid BF1 lock; the lock itself was not falsified. Audit and rerun only any BF1 evidence that actually consumed BF2-generated output, if any.
- **BV0:** candidate `c40a1ca96` and all round-one evidence are obsolete. BV0 must be rebased onto the reaccepted BF2 oracle and evaluated afresh.
- **BF3/BV1/other successors:** invalidate or restack any unaccepted work based on the old BF2 identity. If anything downstream has already been accepted, audit whether its proof consumed affected Vue Vapor/SSR results and reopen only the affected evidence/approval. Preserve independently valid work, as the falsification contract requires ([architecture-falsification contract](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/contracts/architecture-falsification.md:98)).

Operationally, BF2 repair and BV0 correction may be developed as a bounded two-layer stack, but BF2 lands/reaccepts first; BV0 then restacks and revalidates. Governance permits contingent upper work but forbids acceptance before the predecessor lands ([governance](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/governance.md:6)).

## Q2 — Svelte migration

**Ruling: split it out. Mechanical inertness does not create charter authority.**

BV0 is explicitly Vue-only. The architecture review confirms the Svelte migration is independent of the Vue pin, touches production Svelte constants and thousands of corpus/package files, and materially obscures the Vue review surface ([architecture review](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/vue-known-defect-correction/reviews/round1-architecture.md:102)). BF3 was deliberately narrowed to Svelte and non-Vue-runtime work ([BF3 charter](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/charters/BF3.md:23)).

Move the migration to a separate authorized Svelte-domain block/commit. Assign it to BF3 only if a scoped closure review confirms BF3 owns these corpus, dependency, and production-version changes; otherwise create a small dedicated Svelte oracle-alignment block. It must not be folded into BF2 because BF2 is test-only and cannot change production compiler files.

This separation is mandatory even if every output byte is unchanged. Governance says one program block is not co-batched with unrelated changes in its landing delta ([governance](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/governance.md:283)).

docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-475-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-476-## 8.1 Gate immutability
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-477-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md:478:`A6` freezes populated cells in `performance-gates.toml` against the exact post-A3, post-instrumentation implementation baseline before `B1` and every later non-safety foundational cutover. A gate may be recalibrated only when:
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-479-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-480-- machine/toolchain/corpus/competitor changed materially;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-481-- raw before/after calibration is retained;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-482-- no candidate result was inspected before choosing the new threshold;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md:483:- an independent performance reviewer accepts the change;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-484-- the affected block is re-baselined or restarted.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-485-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-486-A candidate cannot choose its pass criterion after measurement.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-487-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-488-## 8.2 Benchmark cell identity
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-489-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-490-Each cell fixes:
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-491-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-492-- source/corpus fingerprint and size distribution;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-493-- framework, product contract, semantic profile, mappings, diagnostics, provenance, serialization;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-494-- direct/prepared/managed state and warm definition;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-495-- threading/execution profile;
--
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-582-A competitor row is valid only when it records:
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-583-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-584-- exact source revision/version and build flags;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-585-- corpus and target equivalence;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-586-- output validity and supported semantics;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-587-- source maps/diagnostics/imported-type behavior;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-588-- threading and cache state;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-589-- boundary included/excluded;
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-590-- raw result and uncertainty.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-591-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-592-Verter must first pass absolute SLO and self no-regression. It is blocking-dominated when one valid competitor is materially faster **and** lower peak RSS under the locked tolerance while doing equivalent work.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-593-
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md:594:The `A6` lock sets exact aggregate and strategic-cell tolerances before candidate implementation. The target is to meet or beat the fastest valid equivalent-work Rust implementation on the primary direct-suite aggregate and to avoid material Pareto domination in strategic cells. A candidate miss is blocking and cannot be waived after results are known. If indispensable extra work or a comparison mismatch proves the locked product/equivalence premise false, the project must amend the product/architecture and Implementation Lock Record under the blind recalibration rule, invalidate affected candidate evidence, and restart. Repeated work, weaker semantics, invalid comparison, or unbounded retention are never acceptable premises.
docs/arch/refactor/rev11/verification.md-595-

# Final-candidate execution (second-reopen fix tree) — BF2_VUE_ORACLE_MANIFEST_GENERATE / BF2_SVELTE_ORACLE_MANIFEST_GENERATE

Real execution evidence for the two locked `performance-gates.toml` cells,
run against the second-reopen FIX candidate (session-recorded
`candidate_head` a78dd9a7f — the exact tree after every fix item landed;
the tree under review differs from it only by this evidence directory and
the final report record). This session REPLACES the prior
`oracle-manifest-cells-final-candidate` session, whose evidence was ruled
invalid (wrong-SHA binding / insufficient exclusivity — concurrent review
activity was running during it). It makes no claim about, and does not
supersede, any cell other than these two; the separate
`BF2_OFFICIAL_COMPILER_INVOCATION_GOLDEN_GENERATE` row remains OPEN under
its existing debt disposition (`../../debt-BF2-perf-gate-deferred.md`).

## Exclusive-lease verification (the invalidation cause, addressed head-on)

Verified IMMEDIATELY before the session start, by direct process
inspection (`ps aux` sorted by CPU, sampled repeatedly over several
minutes), not assumption:

- Zero builds, test suites, benchmarks, or other agent/review sessions
  running. The only other Claude session on the machine was idle
  (intermittent ≤10% of one core, no spawned work).
- Spotlight (`spotlightknowledged.updater`) was initially indexing this
  session's own freshly-created files (bursts to ~50–80% of one core); the
  session was DELAYED until six consecutive 5-second samples read 0.0%.
- Residual disclosed load: `RustDesk` (a resident remote-desktop daemon,
  not stoppable from this session) idling at ~10–30% of ONE core on an
  8-core machine, plus WindowServer/Terminal UI noise. Nothing else above
  20% of a core at any sampled point.
- AC power ("AC attached"), load averages ~1.9/8 cores at session start.

Post-hoc corroboration that the window was genuinely quiet: this
session's wall-time coefficient of variation is 1.2565% — TIGHTER than
the frozen BF1 baseline session's own 2.3388% and the tightest of any
session recorded for these cells. A perturbed session shows the opposite.
- parallel unlocked blocks have disjoint or explicitly coordinated cutover closures.

An edge may be added when reality reveals a dependency. Removing an edge requires an authority amendment, a clean architecture challenge, and re-ratification of every already-open affected charter.

## 4.1 Gate recalibration

A locked threshold/corpus/machine class may change only when the environment or benchmark definition changed materially. Recalibration requires:

1. a written cause unrelated to candidate direction;
2. retained old/new calibration data;
3. a reviewer who has not inspected the candidate result direction where practical;
4. amendment of the Implementation Lock Record;
5. invalidation and rerun of affected candidate evidence.

A threshold is never relaxed because a candidate missed it. A benchmark bug is fixed and both baseline and candidate are rerun.

codex
The correct disposition is **(c), with a scope correction**: these two sessions are neither PASS evidence nor a valid candidate-attributable gate failure. Retain them as nonconforming diagnostic runs, and do not recalibrate or waive the threshold.

## Q1

A valid locked-gate failure blocks regardless of whether the changed diff is the likely cause. Attribution determines the remedy; it does not turn a failed conjunctive metric into a pass.

But these were not valid gate sessions. The locked runner requires “no other user workload,” and the statistics policy voids sessions run while the machine is not idle or is interrupted ([performance-gates.toml](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/performance-gates.toml:59)). The prior BF2 evidence specifically rejected an earlier run for insufficient exclusivity and waited for other sessions and Spotlight to become idle ([reopen-2 evidence](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/evidence/BF2/command-proofs/oracle-manifest-cells-reopen2-fix-final/README.md:7)).

Two runs under the same persistent confounds are reproducible, but not environmentally independent. They reproduce the contention regime, not a workload regression. Session 2 being slightly slower does not rebut contention; scheduling, thermal state, and persistent background CPU are not monotonic.

Therefore:

- Do not declare a genuine locked-gate failure yet.
- Do not declare PASS.
- If these cells are required, BF2 is temporarily `NOT PROVEN` and must not land until a valid disposition exists.

Minor arithmetic correction: the listed reopen-2 samples have a median of **22.810 s**, not 22.855 s. This does not change the conclusion. The authoritative frozen ceiling is approximately **25.3529 s**.

## Q2

Choose **(c)**.

Record both sessions as something like:

> `NONCONFORMING / NOT ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE — locked runner-idleness condition violated by active unrelated workload; raw results retained.`

Do not use option (b). Transparent disclosure is necessary, but disclosure cannot transform an out-of-threshold result into PASS.

Do not proceed directly to option (a), either. Transient host contention is not a material machine/toolchain/corpus change warranting recalibration. Recalibration is maintainer-only, requires an unrelated material premise change, retained old/new calibration, a new lock record, and rerunning affected evidence ([governance.md](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/docs/arch/refactor/rev11/governance.md:167)). A post-result waiver is especially inappropriate because these cells state `post_result_exception_allowed = false`.

There is also an important scoping point: the two cells explicitly measure enumeration/classification, make zero compiler calls, and produce no compiled golden output ([performance-gates.toml](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/performance-gates.toml:420)). On the quoted instruction—“rerun **affected** BF2 performance and provenance gates”—these cells are structurally unaffected by the `invoke-vue-oracle.mjs` repair.

My final scoping ruling is therefore:

- Record an exact identity/diff proof showing these two cells’ complete measured inputs are unchanged.
- Classify them as **outside this reopen’s affected performance cone**, rather than calling the contaminated executions PASS.
- Rerun the genuinely affected golden/provenance gates.
- These two manifest cells should not block landing on the facts supplied.

If a stronger ratified reopen packet explicitly names these two cell IDs as mandatory executions—not merely “affected gates”—that explicit requirement controls. In that case, obtain a valid quiet-window rerun before landing.

## Q3

Yes, retrying later on the **same machine is acceptable and preferable**. Fairness requires eliminating the confounding activity during the measurement window, not necessarily terminating the processes themselves.

Specifically:

- RustDesk need not be killed; it was present during the accepted baseline. It must be idle and within the previously recorded residual range.
- The unrelated Claude process may remain alive, but it must be genuinely idle with no spawned builds/tests/tool work. Its current sustained 14–22% CPU is not acceptable.
- Wait for Spotlight/indexing to quiesce, using the prior six-consecutive-samples procedure.
- Require AC power, low-power mode off, no thermal throttling, and no other user workload.
- Apply the locked start/end control benchmark and 3% drift rule.
- Predeclare those validity conditions before the next attempt. Do not repeatedly rerun until a favorable median appears.

Because the current statistics have already been read, preserve these sessions rather than retroactively “discarding” them. The next attempt should be explicitly registered as a new acceptance session under restored runner conditions.

If a fully conforming quiet-window session still exceeds 25.3529 s, then STOP. At that point the unchanged measured subject strongly indicates historical runner/environment drift rather than a BF2 code regression, but landing still requires a formal maintainer/performance-authority ruling or compliant recalibration—not an orchestrator waiver or threshold edit.

A post-result waiver is especially inappropriate because these cells state `post_result_exception_allowed = false`.

There is also an important scoping point: the two cells explicitly measure enumeration/classification, make zero compiler calls, and produce no compiled golden output ([performance-gates.toml](/Users/carlosrodrigues/Documents/dev/verter/performance-gates.toml:420)). On the quoted instruction—“rerun **affected** BF2 performance and provenance gates”—these cells are structurally unaffected by the `invoke-vue-oracle.mjs` repair.

My final scoping ruling is therefore:

- Record an exact identity/diff proof showing these two cells’ complete measured inputs are unchanged.
- Classify them as **outside this reopen’s affected performance cone**, rather than calling the contaminated executions PASS.
- Rerun the genuinely affected golden/provenance gates.
- These two manifest cells should not block landing on the facts supplied.

If a stronger ratified reopen packet explicitly names these two cell IDs as mandatory executions—not merely “affected gates”—that explicit requirement controls. In that case, obtain a valid quiet-window rerun before landing.

## Q3

Yes, retrying later on the **same machine is acceptable and preferable**. Fairness requires eliminating the confounding activity during the measurement window, not necessarily terminating the processes themselves.

Specifically:

- RustDesk need not be killed; it was present during the accepted baseline. It must be idle and within the previously recorded residual range.
- The unrelated Claude process may remain alive, but it must be genuinely idle with no spawned builds/tests/tool work. Its current sustained 14–22% CPU is not acceptable.
- Wait for Spotlight/indexing to quiesce, using the prior six-consecutive-samples procedure.
- Require AC power, low-power mode off, no thermal throttling, and no other user workload.
- Apply the locked start/end control benchmark and 3% drift rule.
- Predeclare those validity conditions before the next attempt. Do not repeatedly rerun until a favorable median appears.

Because the current statistics have already been read, preserve these sessions rather than retroactively “discarding” them. The next attempt should be explicitly registered as a new acceptance session under restored runner conditions.

If a fully conforming quiet-window session still exceeds 25.3529 s, then STOP. At that point the unchanged measured subject strongly indicates historical runner/environment drift rather than a BF2 code regression, but landing still requires a formal maintainer/performance-authority ruling or compliant recalibration—not an orchestrator waiver or threshold edit.
